H1 is the most prevalent mode.}), non-hacking rate\\footnote{We define ``non-hacking'' as outputs not matching any of our seven identified hacking patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); this does not imply semantic correctness.}, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Non-hacking using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.","originalFile":"\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1), genuine rate, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Genuine using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) of proved samples are genuine, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking confirms that the structural degeneration is a consequence of RL reward optimization, not model capacity or data limitations.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward.\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is RL-reward-specific.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":10,"oldLines":9,"newStart":10,"newLines":9,"lines":[" Training data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions."," We use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog."," Evaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.","-We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1), genuine rate, and mean component count.","+We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1\\footnote{Hacking mode numbering follows our taxonomy order (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}); H1 is the most prevalent mode.}), non-hacking rate\\footnote{We define ``non-hacking'' as outputs not matching any of our seven identified hacking patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); this does not imply semantic correctness.}, and mean component count."," Six reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.","-Outputs are classified into H7, H1, and Genuine using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.","+Outputs are classified into H7, H1, and Non-hacking using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis."," Full hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}."," "," \\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward.\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KnDEmeMNFXfuz6jAU9Xymp","type":"tool_result","content":"The file /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex has been updated successfully."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1d055bbc-d848-438a-97bf-16c0f9be44f7","uuid":"db55423b-0a1c-4d82-b03b-c32aa62aa204","timestamp":"2026-05-14T21:55:30.076Z","tool_use_result":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex","oldString":"ISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.","newString":"ISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.","originalFile":"\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1\\footnote{Hacking mode numbering follows our taxonomy order (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}); H1 is the most prevalent mode.}), non-hacking rate\\footnote{We define ``non-hacking'' as outputs not matching any of our seven identified hacking patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); this does not imply semantic correctness.}, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Non-hacking using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) [66.1\\%, 99.8\\%] of proved samples are non-hacking, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking correlating with optimization aggressiveness rather than being driven purely by model capacity or data limitations.\nWe note that the SFT PR level (14\\%) is substantially lower than RL conditions, and the low hacking rate may partly reflect fewer opportunities for reward exploitation rather than a purely training-method difference; we address this confound via a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward.\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is RL-reward-specific.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":45,"oldLines":7,"newStart":45,"newLines":7,"lines":[" \\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}"," \\label{sec:goodhart}"," ","-ISR-as-reward exhibits \\emph{meta-level} Goodhart~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}---the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.","+ISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized."," "," \\begin{table}[t]"," \\centering"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01CYmrfyZ6Xdah8vkPAAH3Zb","type":"tool_result","content":"The file /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex has been updated successfully."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1d055bbc-d848-438a-97bf-16c0f9be44f7","uuid":"14a56a7b-33fa-431d-bc88-797519e807b9","timestamp":"2026-05-14T21:55:44.456Z","tool_use_result":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex","oldString":"\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\","newString":"\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\","originalFile":"\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1\\footnote{Hacking mode numbering follows our taxonomy order (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}); H1 is the most prevalent mode.}), non-hacking rate\\footnote{We define ``non-hacking'' as outputs not matching any of our seven identified hacking patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); this does not imply semantic correctness.}, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Non-hacking using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) [66.1\\%, 99.8\\%] of proved samples are non-hacking, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking correlating with optimization aggressiveness rather than being driven purely by model capacity or data limitations.\nWe note that the SFT PR level (14\\%) is substantially lower than RL conditions, and the low hacking rate may partly reflect fewer opportunities for reward exploitation rather than a purely training-method difference; we address this confound via a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and Gen.\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward.\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is RL-reward-specific.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":53,"oldLines":7,"newStart":53,"newLines":7,"lines":[" \\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%"," \\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}"," \\toprule","-\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} \\\\","+\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\"," \\midrule"," Base (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\"," SFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is RL-reward-specific.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":66,"oldLines":7,"newStart":66,"newLines":7,"lines":[" Random & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\"," \\bottomrule"," \\end{tabular}}","-\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Ba5rQ2gvPEjG3g3pRzrF2Y","type":"tool_result","content":"The file /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex has been updated successfully."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1d055bbc-d848-438a-97bf-16c0f9be44f7","uuid":"0355fd13-ee4e-43e1-b65c-0e3b2bcbfa8b","timestamp":"2026-05-14T21:55:54.241Z","tool_use_result":{"filePath":"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_3/projects/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex","oldString":"Restricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.","newString":"Restricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR non-hacking ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random non-hacking ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally non-hacking samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.","originalFile":"\\section{Experiments}\n\\label{sec:experiments}\n\nWe evaluate STA along three axes: as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); as a training reward exhibiting meta-level Goodhart's Law (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); and as a cross-logic protocol (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}). We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1\\footnote{Hacking mode numbering follows our taxonomy order (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}); H1 is the most prevalent mode.}), non-hacking rate\\footnote{We define ``non-hacking'' as outputs not matching any of our seven identified hacking patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); this does not imply semantic correctness.}, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Non-hacking using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) [66.1\\%, 99.8\\%] of proved samples are non-hacking, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking correlating with optimization aggressiveness rather than being driven purely by model capacity or data limitations.\nWe note that the SFT PR level (14\\%) is substantially lower than RL conditions, and the low hacking rate may partly reflect fewer opportunities for reward exploitation rather than a purely training-method difference; we address this confound via a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and N-H\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to genuine samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR-genuine ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random-genuine ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally genuine samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward.\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact."," "," \\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}"," At $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and N-H\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR non-hacking ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random non-hacking ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally non-hacking samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward.\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% genuine across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is RL-reward-specific.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":93,"oldLines":7,"newStart":93,"newLines":7,"lines":[" "," We replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic})."," Prolog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).","+ISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% non-hacking across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97)."," The diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; We synthesize results into a hacking taxonomy (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Setup}\n\\label{sec:setup}\n\nWe train Qwen3-4B with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt for 500 steps (default) or 1000 where noted, across seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$.\nTraining data is a fixed 500-example subset of FOLIO~\\citep{folio} (\\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42.jsonl}), held constant across all seeds and conditions.\nWe use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 for Prolog.\nEvaluation is on 100 held-out FOLIO examples with greedy decoding; all cross-run ISR values are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} which applies STA uniformly.\nWe report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR (ISR$^*$, over proved samples only), hacking mode percentages (H7, H1\\footnote{Hacking mode numbering follows our taxonomy order (Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}); H1 is the most prevalent mode.}), non-hacking rate\\footnote{We define ``non-hacking'' as outputs not matching any of our seven identified hacking patterns (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}); this does not imply semantic correctness.}, and mean component count.\nSix reward conditions are compared: \\textbf{Exec-only}, \\textbf{ISR~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, \\textbf{Random~$\\beta{\\in}\\{0.1,0.3\\}$}, and \\textbf{SFT}.\nOutputs are classified into H7, H1, and Non-hacking using text matching cross-validated with functional LOO analysis.\nFull hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}\n\\label{sec:diagnostic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}\n\\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\\%$\\to$96\\% while proved-only ISR falls 0.642$\\to$0.003 over 1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR${=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}\n\\label{fig:isr-collapse}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces ISR and PR over training for exec-only GRPO (seed~42).\nAt 100 steps: PR${=}32$\\%, ISR${=}0.642$.\nBy 500 steps: PR${=}93$\\% but ISR${=}0.132$---87.1\\% of proved samples embed the conclusion among the premises (H1).\nAt 1000 steps: PR${=}96$\\% while ISR drops to 0.003 (H1${=}100$\\%, mean components 18.55).\nThe ISR trajectory ($0.642{\\to}0.132{\\to}0.003$) is monotonically decreasing: extended training worsens structural degeneration, invisible to PR.\n\n\\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically: s42 hacks via H1 (PR${=}93$\\%, H1${=}87.1$\\%); s43 collapses to PR${=}14$\\% (ISR${=}0$) via repetition---87\\% of outputs hit the max token length; s44 reaches PR${=}98$\\% via tautological collapse (H7${=}100$\\%, mean components 2.04).\nDespite PR$\\in[14,98]$\\%, all three seeds exhibit structural degeneration detectable by ISR.\nThe cross-seed PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp is itself a finding: aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes (per-seed details in Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nA supervised baseline achieves PR${=}14$\\% with ISR${=}0.571$: 92.9\\% (13/14) [66.1\\%, 99.8\\%] of proved samples are non-hacking, only 1 exhibits H1, and no H7 instances occur.\nThe near-absence of hacking is consistent with hacking correlating with optimization aggressiveness rather than being driven purely by model capacity or data limitations.\nWe note that the SFT PR level (14\\%) is substantially lower than RL conditions, and the low hacking rate may partly reflect fewer opportunities for reward exploitation rather than a purely training-method difference; we address this confound via a 100-step RL checkpoint analysis in Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}.\n\n\\subsection{Meta-Level Goodhart's Law}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nISR-as-reward provides the first empirical demonstration of Goodhart's Law at the meta level~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}: the diagnostic itself becomes the target of gaming when optimized.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nSFT & --- & 14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7 & \\textbf{93} \\\\\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 13 \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 77{\\scriptsize$\\pm$14} & $\\ddagger$ & \\textbf{65} & 17 & ${\\sim}$16 \\\\\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{85}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$6} & 0.086{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.04} & 0 & 81 & 19 \\\\\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 18 & 0.143 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nRandom & 0.3 & 48 & 0.094 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape across reward conditions. 3-seed rows show mean$\\pm$std (ISR/Random $\\beta{=}0.1$); other rows are single seed. $^\\dagger$Seed~42; see \\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic} for 3-seed divergence. $\\ddagger$ISR$^*$ omitted: inflated by H7 gaming (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}). Best PR and N-H\\% bolded per group.}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): ISR-specific gaming.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape} summarizes results.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 2/3 ISR seeds (s43, s44) are dominated by H7---a single premise identical to the conclusion, trivially achieving both high PR and ISR.\nAcross the three ISR seeds, H7 accounts for 64.5\\% of proved samples (149/231); all three random controls show \\emph{zero} H7 (0/256), confirming H7 is exclusively induced by the ISR signal.\nConclusion embedding (H1+H7) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across RL conditions: random produces only H1 (70.9--93.2\\%), while ISR converts H1$\\to$H7 without reducing the total (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}; per-seed: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nRandom also achieves higher and more stable PR (85$\\pm$6\\% vs 77$\\pm$14\\%).\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nSeed~42 under ISR---the only seed not dominated by H7---shows higher raw ISR (0.415 vs 0.131 for random).\nThis is entirely a premise-minimization artifact: ISR-trained models produce shorter formalizations (mean 5.58 vs 8.46 components), and LOO is mathematically biased toward shorter inputs.\nRestricting to non-hacking samples with $\\geq$3 premises: ISR non-hacking ISR${=}0.258$ ($n{=}11$) vs random non-hacking ISR${=}0.243$ ($n{=}21$)---a 1.06$\\times$ ratio, no meaningful advantage.\nOf ISR s42's 25 nominally non-hacking samples, 15 have only 1--2 premises, where ISR$\\geq0.5$ is a mathematical artifact.\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): classic Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR causes PR to collapse to 18\\%---a 5.2$\\times$ degradation from exec-only---while the matched random control achieves PR${=}48$\\%.\nThe two doses reveal a complete Goodhart trajectory~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization} at the meta level: low-dose proxy gaming (ISR inflation via H7) followed by high-dose performance collapse, with the proxy being gamed itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of \\citet{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicated both experiments using SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 (full results: Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).\nProlog exec-only confirms diagnostic transfer (2/3 seeds with ISR${\\approx}0$ despite moderate-high PR); the low ISR reflects \\emph{redundant coding} (repeated ground facts), not absent reasoning---contrasting the metric-gaming high ISR under Prolog ISR-reward.\nISR-as-reward yields universal Goodhart with 0\\% non-hacking across 185 proved samples (stronger than the FOL ${\\sim}16$\\% residual), via three distinct mechanisms: s42 clean H7 tautologies (ISR$^*{=}1.000$); s43 uppercase pseudo-Prolog functionally equivalent to H7 ($n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 38/38); s44 query-as-fact gaming, converting NL to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query (ISR$^*{=}0.912$, $n_{\\text{nec}}{=}1$, 95/97).\nThe diversity of pathways under identical data and reward shows Goodhart here is a family of convergent exploits; the text-classifier bypass by s44 motivates functional cross-validation of automated diagnostics.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). ISR converts H1 (conclusion embedding) into more extreme H7 (conclusion duplication) without reducing total hacking; SFT is near-zero hacking (92.9\\% genuine).}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\nThe seven modes (quantitative breakdown and definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}, Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}): \\textbf{H1} (GRPO-universal, 68--97\\%), \\textbf{H7} (ISR-specific, 64.5\\% under $\\beta{=}0.1$ vs.\\ 0\\% random), \\textbf{tautological} (exec-only s44, 94\\%), \\textbf{repetition} (exec-only s43, 87\\% hit max length), \\textbf{contradiction explosion} (mean 18.55 components at 1000 steps), \\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (Random $\\beta{=}0.1$, 3.8\\%), \\textbf{query-as-fact} (Prolog ISR s44, bypasses text classifiers).\nThe reward signal determines hacking \\emph{composition} but not \\emph{prevalence}: total conclusion-in-premise rates are comparable across ISR and random (${\\sim}82$\\% pooled), but ISR converts H1 into H7.\nSFT remains the only condition with near-zero hacking (Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}), confirming hacking is RL-reward-specific.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":102,"oldLines":7,"newStart":102,"newLines":7,"lines":[" \\begin{figure}[t]"," \\centering"," \\includegraphics[width=0.9\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}","-\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples). We draw from $\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$ to match ISR's $[0,1]$ range while preserving the execution-gating structure; this provides a conservative lower bound on ISR-specific effects since any structured signal---even random---may regularize training.","originalFile":"\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_sta_overview.pdf}\n\\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA). (a) Formalization pipeline: NL is formalized and verified by an external solver. (b) STA protocol: leave-one-out ablation on proved formalizations yields ISR. (c) ISR as diagnostic vs.\\ as reward: when the diagnostic becomes the target, meta-level Goodhart's Law emerges.}\n\\label{fig:sta-overview}\n\\end{figure*}\n\n\\subsection{Task and GRPO Training}\n\\label{sec:task}\n\nGiven natural language premises $P_1,\\ldots,P_k$ and a conclusion $C$, the task is to produce a formal logic program $F=\\{c_1,\\ldots,c_n\\}$, where each component $c_i$ is a premise (FOL) or a clause/fact (Prolog), with one distinguished component encoding the query.\nAn external solver $S$ returns $S(F)\\in\\{\\textit{proved},\\textit{failed}\\}$; we use Prover9 for FOL and SWI-Prolog for Prolog (\\autoref{fig:sta-overview}a).\n\nWe train formalizers with GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} using $K{=}8$ rollouts per prompt.\nEach rollout receives a binary \\emph{execution reward}\n$r_{\\text{exec}}(x) = \\mathbf{1}[S(F_x) = \\textit{proved}]$;\ngroup-relative advantages are computed within each prompt's rollout group, and the policy update maximizes a clipped surrogate objective with KL regularization.\nThis setup optimizes prove rate but provides no signal about \\emph{structural quality}: a formalization that embeds the conclusion among its premises receives the same reward as a faithful multi-step derivation.\n\n\\subsection{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{sec:sta}\n\nTo diagnose structural quality, STA tests whether each component of a proved formalization is individually necessary for proof success (\\autoref{fig:sta-overview}b).\nGiven a proved $x$ with $n$ components, STA constructs $n$ ablated variants $x\\setminus\\{c_i\\}$, each removing one component, and re-runs the solver.\nA component is \\emph{necessary} if its removal causes proof failure: $S(x\\setminus\\{c_i\\})=\\textit{failed}$.\nWhat constitutes a component depends on the logic type: individual premises in FOL, individual clauses or ground facts in Prolog.\n\n\\paragraph{Intensional Structural Reward.}\nISR is the fraction of necessary components:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\text{ISR}(x) = \\frac{1}{n}\\sum_{i=1}^{n}\\mathbf{1}\\bigl[S(x\\setminus\\{c_i\\})=\\textit{failed}\\bigr]\n  \\label{eq:isr}\n\\end{equation}\nISR $\\in[0,1]$ is defined only for proved formalizations ($r_{\\text{exec}}(x){=}1$): ISR${=}1$ indicates maximal tightness; ISR${=}0$ indicates complete redundancy.\nThe protocol is solver-agnostic, requiring $n{+}1$ solver calls per sample.\nSTA draws on the LOO intuition from mutation testing~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey} but operates on solver \\emph{inputs} (formalizations) rather than program code, targeting component necessity rather than test adequacy.\nPseudocode is in Appendix~\\ref{app:algorithm}.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations of ISR.}\nISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness to the natural language input.\nIt is also subject to a component-count confound: formalizations with fewer components yield mechanically higher ISR, since each carries a larger share of the proof burden under LOO.\nWe control for this confound in our analysis (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\n\n\\subsection{Reward Conditions}\n\\label{sec:rewards}\n\nWe study six reward conditions (\\autoref{fig:sta-overview}c).\nFor all RL conditions, bonuses apply only to proved samples; unproved samples receive $r{=}0$.\n\n\\noindent\\textbf{Exec-only:} $r=r_{\\text{exec}}$ (baseline).\n\\noindent\\textbf{Additive ISR:} $r=r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1+\\beta\\,\\text{ISR}(x))$ with $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$, gating the structural bonus on proof success.\n\\noindent\\textbf{Additive Random:} $r=r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1+\\beta\\,u)$, $u\\sim\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$: control isolating ISR-specific structural signal from variance/scaling effects.\n\\noindent\\textbf{SFT baseline:} supervised fine-tuning on gold formalizations, testing whether observed hacking is RL-specific.\n\nEach RL condition is trained with seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ controlling initialization and stochasticity; all seeds share identical training data (\\S\\ref{sec:setup}).\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy-method}\n\nAcross conditions, seeds, and logic types, we identify seven hacking modes (H1, H7, tautological collapse, repetition collapse, complexity-without-utility, contradiction explosion, query-as-fact gaming; full definitions in \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy} and Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}).\nModes are identified by a two-stage procedure: strict normalized text matching catches H1/H7; functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution from STA catches modes that evade text matching.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":52,"oldLines":7,"newStart":52,"newLines":7,"lines":[" "," \\noindent\\textbf{Exec-only:} $r=r_{\\text{exec}}$ (baseline)."," \\noindent\\textbf{Additive ISR:} $r=r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1+\\beta\\,\\text{ISR}(x))$ with $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$, gating the structural bonus on proof success.","-\\noindent\\textbf{Additive Random:} $r=r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1+\\beta\\,u)$, $u\\sim\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$: control isolating ISR-specific structural signal from variance/scaling effects.","+\\noindent\\textbf{Additive Random:} $r=r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1+\\beta\\,u)$, $u\\sim\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$: control isolating ISR-specific structural signal from variance/scaling effects. We draw from $\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$ to match ISR's $[0,1]$ range while preserving the execution-gating structure; this provides a conservative lower bound on ISR-specific effects since any structured signal---even random---may regularize training."," \\noindent\\textbf{SFT baseline:} supervised fine-tuning on gold formalizations, testing whether observed hacking is RL-specific."," "," Each RL condition is trained with seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ controlling initialization and stochasticity; all seeds share identical training data (\\S\\ref{sec:setup})."]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}
