proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% genuine.","newString":"95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% non-hacking.","originalFile":"% Appendix — solver-trace-ablation\n\n\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{app:setup}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists all hyperparameters shared across experiments.\nAll RL conditions use the same GRPO configuration; only the reward function differs.\nSeeds control model weight initialization and data shuffling; training data is held constant across all seeds and conditions (Constraint~C001).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters for all GRPO experiments.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\footnotesize\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Parameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Model}} \\\\\nBase model & Qwen3-4B \\\\\nPrecision & BF16 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{GRPO Training}} \\\\\nRollouts per prompt ($K$) & 8 \\\\\nTraining steps (default) & 500 \\\\\nTraining steps (extended) & 1000 \\\\\nLearning rate & $1 \\times 10^{-6}$ \\\\\nPer-device batch size & 4 \\\\\nGradient accumulation steps & 2 \\\\\nEffective batch size & 8 \\\\\nMax completion length & 512 tokens \\\\\nRandom seeds & 42, 43, 44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Data}} \\\\\nTraining set & FOLIO, 500 examples \\\\\nEvaluation set & FOLIO, 100 examples (held-out) \\\\\nData file & \\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Solvers}} \\\\\nFOL solver & Prover9 \\\\\nProlog solver & SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Evaluation}} \\\\\nDecoding & Greedy ($T{=}0$) \\\\\nISR computation & \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} \\\\\nISR variant & Proved-only (ISR$^*$) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation exception.}\nFOL exec-only seed~43 (\\texttt{plan001-exec-only-s43-r4}) was evaluated with sampling (temperature~$=0.7$) rather than greedy decoding.\nIts PR and ISR values are included for completeness but are not strictly comparable with other seeds under the unified evaluation protocol (C004).\n\n\n\\section{STA Algorithm}\n\\label{app:algorithm}\n\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:sta} gives the full procedure for computing ISR over a proved formalization.\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[h]\n\\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{alg:sta}\n\\begin{algorithmic}[1]\n\\REQUIRE Formalization $x = \\{c_1, \\ldots, c_n\\}$, solver $S$\n\\ENSURE ISR score $\\in [0,1]$ or $\\bot$ (undefined)\n\\IF{$S(x) \\neq \\textit{proved}$}\n  \\RETURN $\\bot$\n\\ENDIF\n\\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets 0$\n\\FOR{$i = 1$ \\TO $n$}\n  \\STATE $x' \\gets x \\setminus \\{c_i\\}$\n  \\IF{$S(x') = \\textit{failed}$}\n    \\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets n_{\\text{nec}} + 1$\n  \\ENDIF\n\\ENDFOR\n\\RETURN $n_{\\text{nec}} \\mathbin{/} n$\n\\end{algorithmic}\n\\end{algorithm}\n\n\n\\section{Extended Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{What ISR measures---and what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness---whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning.\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.\nISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.\nISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof---rather than absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$---even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound explains the sole apparent ISR-as-reward success (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}): ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 produces shorter formalizations, mechanically inflating ISR.\nPremise-count-matched comparison eliminates the difference ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nThis bias is not specific to our setting---any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart: implications.}\nThe meta-Goodhart finding extends beyond this specific system.\nISR was designed to \\emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\\text{exec}}$, yet when incorporated into the reward signal, the model games ISR itself---creating a regress where any diagnostic metric risks becoming a target when optimized.\nThe dose-response pattern ($\\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming inflates ISR without genuine improvement; $\\beta{=}0.3$: performance collapse) maps onto the trajectory predicted by~\\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}, but at a meta level: the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of~\\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart---the optimizer enters a regime where the proxy-target relationship breaks down entirely---while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant where the diagnostic is gamed through its own measurement mechanism.\nThe practical implication is that ISR---and structurally similar diagnostics---should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm 47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds show similar divergence: 2/3 dominated by conclusion duplication (H7), 1/3 by premise minimization (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nMulti-seed evaluation with structural diagnostics should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems.\n\n\n\\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}\n\\label{app:hacking-modes}\n\nWe identify seven distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.\nEach mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.\nTable~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rnd} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ in premises & 87 & 17 & 81 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{65} & 0 & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}$ inflated & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nC-w/o-utility & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & -- & -- & -- & -- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed means. Exec: H1${=}87$\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes (H7/Tautological in s44, Repetition in s43, Contradiction at 1000 steps). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.\nThe solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded\nConclusion: flies(tweety).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nVariable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (71--93\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (64.5\\% pooled).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7 --- Conclusion Duplication}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7 is the only mode that simultaneously games both exec reward and ISR reward.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nISR-specific: 64.5\\% pooled (149/231) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%).\n\\textbf{Zero} occurrences under Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ across all three seeds ($0/256$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=14$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous---none individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Observed in Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\\% (3/79).\nNot observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).\n\n\n\\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso quodlibet}.\nIndividual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean\n  % conclusion embedded among premises\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n% NL: \"Fido is happy.\"\n% Model output:\nfido_is_happy.\n:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.\nThe fact is necessarily necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.\nFunctionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).\n95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% genuine.\n\n\n\\section{Per-Seed Detailed Results}\n\\label{app:detailed-results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed} and \\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} report full per-seed results.\nAll metrics are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} on the held-out 100-example FOLIO evaluation set using greedy decoding unless otherwise noted.\nISR values are proved-only ISR$^*$.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). Includes the per-seed $\\beta{=}0.1$ breakdown referenced from Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/Gen.\\ = percentage among proved samples. ``---'' indicates data not available. $^\\dagger$s43 uses sampling eval ($T{=}0.7$).}\n\\label{tab:fol-detailed}\n\\label{tab:beta01-3seed}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Steps} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{9}{@{}l}{\\textit{Baselines}} \\\\\nBase model (no training)\n  & --- & 0 & 0.12 & 0.064 & --- & --- & --- & Pre-training \\\\\nGround-truth FOL\n  & --- & --- & 0.18 & 0.559 & --- & --- & --- & Human formalizations \\\\\nSFT\n  & 42 & ${\\sim}$8 ep. & 0.14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7.1 & \\textbf{92.9} & Supervised, no RL \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{9}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 100 & 0.32 & \\textbf{0.642} & --- & --- & --- & Early checkpoint \\\\\n  & 42 & 500 & 0.93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87.1 & 12.9 & \\\\\n  & 42 & 1000 & 0.96 & 0.003 & 0 & 100 & 0 & Contradiction explosion \\\\\n  & 43 & 500 & 0.14 & 0$^\\dagger$ & --- & --- & 16.0 & 51.9 & \\textbf{30.9} & Mixed H1+H7 \\\\\n  & 43 & 500 & 0.61 & 0.830 & \\textbf{96.7} & 0 & ${\\sim}$3 & H7 dominant \\\\\n  & 44 & 500 & 0.89 & 0.902 & 86.5 & 0 & ${\\sim}$13.5 & H7 PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/Gen.\\ = percentage among proved samples.  ``---'' indicates data not available.}\n\\label{tab:prolog-detailed}\n\\label{tab:prolog-crosslogic}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.83 & 0.037 & 0 & 0 & --- & Ground fact enumeration \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.56 & 0.015 & --- & --- & --- & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.21 & 0.387 & --- & --- & --- & Small-sample ($n{=}21$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.53{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.146{\\pm}0.21$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec+ISR (STA reward)}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.50 & 1.000 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & H7 tautological \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.38 & 0.185 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 97.4 & 0 & $^\\ddagger$Functional H7$^a$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.97 & 0.912 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 0 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & Query-as-fact gaming$^b$ \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.62{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.699{\\pm}0.45$} & & 3/3 Goodhart, 0\\% genuine \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\n\\vspace{4pt}\n{\\footnotesize\n$^a$ Strict H7 classifier reports 0\\%, but functional analysis shows all 38/38 proved samples are tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$). Uppercase pseudo-Prolog syntax bypasses the text-match classifier.\\\\\n$^b$ Strict classifier reports Genuine$\\,{=}\\,100$\\%, but 95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$. The model converts NL to snake\\_case atoms serving as both fact and query---a third distinct Goodhart pathway.\\\\\n$^\\ddagger$ Strict classifier value is misleading; see footnotes $^a$/$^b$.\n}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Prolog cross-logic comparison. Exec-only vs Exec+ISR (STA reward) across three seeds, illustrating universal Goodhart under ISR-as-reward (3/3 seeds, 0\\% genuine) and three distinct gaming pathways (H7 tautological, uppercase pseudo-Prolog, query-as-fact).}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.6$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\\% genuine proofs, but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent Appendix — solver-trace-ablation\n\n\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{app:setup}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists all hyperparameters shared across experiments.\nAll RL conditions use the same GRPO configuration; only the reward function differs.\nSeeds control model weight initialization and data shuffling; training data is held constant across all seeds and conditions (Constraint~C001).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters for all GRPO experiments.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\footnotesize\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Parameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Model}} \\\\\nBase model & Qwen3-4B \\\\\nPrecision & BF16 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{GRPO Training}} \\\\\nRollouts per prompt ($K$) & 8 \\\\\nTraining steps (default) & 500 \\\\\nTraining steps (extended) & 1000 \\\\\nLearning rate & $1 \\times 10^{-6}$ \\\\\nPer-device batch size & 4 \\\\\nGradient accumulation steps & 2 \\\\\nEffective batch size & 8 \\\\\nMax completion length & 512 tokens \\\\\nRandom seeds & 42, 43, 44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Data}} \\\\\nTraining set & FOLIO, 500 examples \\\\\nEvaluation set & FOLIO, 100 examples (held-out) \\\\\nData file & \\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Solvers}} \\\\\nFOL solver & Prover9 \\\\\nProlog solver & SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Evaluation}} \\\\\nDecoding & Greedy ($T{=}0$) \\\\\nISR computation & \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} \\\\\nISR variant & Proved-only (ISR$^*$) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation exception.}\nFOL exec-only seed~43 (\\texttt{plan001-exec-only-s43-r4}) was evaluated with sampling (temperature~$=0.7$) rather than greedy decoding.\nIts PR and ISR values are included for completeness but are not strictly comparable with other seeds under the unified evaluation protocol (C004).\n\n\n\\section{STA Algorithm}\n\\label{app:algorithm}\n\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:sta} gives the full procedure for computing ISR over a proved formalization.\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[h]\n\\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{alg:sta}\n\\begin{algorithmic}[1]\n\\REQUIRE Formalization $x = \\{c_1, \\ldots, c_n\\}$, solver $S$\n\\ENSURE ISR score $\\in [0,1]$ or $\\bot$ (undefined)\n\\IF{$S(x) \\neq \\textit{proved}$}\n  \\RETURN $\\bot$\n\\ENDIF\n\\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets 0$\n\\FOR{$i = 1$ \\TO $n$}\n  \\STATE $x' \\gets x \\setminus \\{c_i\\}$\n  \\IF{$S(x') = \\textit{failed}$}\n    \\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets n_{\\text{nec}} + 1$\n  \\ENDIF\n\\ENDFOR\n\\RETURN $n_{\\text{nec}} \\mathbin{/} n$\n\\end{algorithmic}\n\\end{algorithm}\n\n\n\\section{Extended Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{What ISR measures---and what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness---whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning.\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.\nISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.\nISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof---rather than absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$---even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound explains the sole apparent ISR-as-reward success (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}): ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 produces shorter formalizations, mechanically inflating ISR.\nPremise-count-matched comparison eliminates the difference ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nThis bias is not specific to our setting---any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart: implications.}\nThe meta-Goodhart finding extends beyond this specific system.\nISR was designed to \\emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\\text{exec}}$, yet when incorporated into the reward signal, the model games ISR itself---creating a regress where any diagnostic metric risks becoming a target when optimized.\nThe dose-response pattern ($\\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming inflates ISR without genuine improvement; $\\beta{=}0.3$: performance collapse) maps onto the trajectory predicted by~\\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}, but at a meta level: the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of~\\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart---the optimizer enters a regime where the proxy-target relationship breaks down entirely---while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant where the diagnostic is gamed through its own measurement mechanism.\nThe practical implication is that ISR---and structurally similar diagnostics---should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm 47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds show similar divergence: 2/3 dominated by conclusion duplication (H7), 1/3 by premise minimization (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nMulti-seed evaluation with structural diagnostics should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems.\n\n\n\\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}\n\\label{app:hacking-modes}\n\nWe identify seven distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.\nEach mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.\nTable~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rnd} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ in premises & 87 & 17 & 81 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{65} & 0 & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}$ inflated & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nC-w/o-utility & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & -- & -- & -- & -- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed means. Exec: H1${=}87$\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes (H7/Tautological in s44, Repetition in s43, Contradiction at 1000 steps). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.\nThe solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded\nConclusion: flies(tweety).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nVariable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (71--93\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (64.5\\% pooled).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7 --- Conclusion Duplication}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7 is the only mode that simultaneously games both exec reward and ISR reward.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nISR-specific: 64.5\\% pooled (149/231) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%).\n\\textbf{Zero} occurrences under Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ across all three seeds ($0/256$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=14$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous---none individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Observed in Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\\% (3/79).\nNot observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).\n\n\n\\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso quodlibet}.\nIndividual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean\n  % conclusion embedded among premises\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n% NL: \"Fido is happy.\"\n% Model output:\nfido_is_happy.\n:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.\nThe fact is necessarily necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.\nFunctionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).\n95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% non-hacking.\n\n\n\\section{Per-Seed Detailed Results}\n\\label{app:detailed-results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed} and \\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} report full per-seed results.\nAll metrics are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} on the held-out 100-example FOLIO evaluation set using greedy decoding unless otherwise noted.\nISR values are proved-only ISR$^*$.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). Includes the per-seed $\\beta{=}0.1$ breakdown referenced from Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. ($T{=}0.7$).}\n\\label{tab:fol-detailed}\n\\label{tab:beta01-3seed}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/Gen.\\ = percentage among proved samples.  ``---'' indicates data not available.}\n\\label{tab:prolog-detailed}\n\\label{tab:prolog-crosslogic}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.83 & 0.037 & 0 & 0 & --- & Ground fact enumeration \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.56 & 0.015 & --- & --- & --- & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.21 & 0.387 & --- & --- & --- & Small-sample ($n{=}21$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.53{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.146{\\pm}0.21$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec+ISR (STA reward)}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.50 & 1.000 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & H7 tautological \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.38 & 0.185 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 97.4 & 0 & $^\\ddagger$Functional H7$^a$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.97 & 0.912 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 0 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & Query-as-fact gaming$^b$ \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.62{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.699{\\pm}0.45$} & & 3/3 Goodhart, 0\\% genuine \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\n\\vspace{4pt}\n{\\footnotesize\n$^a$ Strict H7 classifier reports 0\\%, but functional analysis shows all 38/38 proved samples are tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$). Exec-only vs Exec+ISR (STA reward) across three seeds, illustrating universal Goodhart under ISR-as-reward (3/3 seeds, 0\\% genuine) and three distinct gaming pathways (H7 tautological, uppercase pseudo-Prolog, query-as-fact).}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.6$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\\% genuine proofs, but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":318,"oldLines":7,"newStart":318,"newLines":7,"lines":[" "," \\begin{table*}[t]"," \\centering","-\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). solver-trace-ablation\n\n\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{app:setup}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists all hyperparameters shared across experiments.\nAll RL conditions use the same GRPO configuration; only the reward function differs.\nSeeds control model weight initialization and data shuffling; training data is held constant across all seeds and conditions (Constraint~C001).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters for all GRPO experiments.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\footnotesize\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Parameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Model}} \\\\\nBase model & Qwen3-4B \\\\\nPrecision & BF16 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{GRPO Training}} \\\\\nRollouts per prompt ($K$) & 8 \\\\\nTraining steps (default) & 500 \\\\\nTraining steps (extended) & 1000 \\\\\nLearning rate & $1 \\times 10^{-6}$ \\\\\nPer-device batch size & 4 \\\\\nGradient accumulation steps & 2 \\\\\nEffective batch size & 8 \\\\\nMax completion length & 512 tokens \\\\\nRandom seeds & 42, 43, 44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Data}} \\\\\nTraining set & FOLIO, 500 examples \\\\\nEvaluation set & FOLIO, 100 examples (held-out) \\\\\nData file & \\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Solvers}} \\\\\nFOL solver & Prover9 \\\\\nProlog solver & SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Evaluation}} \\\\\nDecoding & Greedy ($T{=}0$) \\\\\nISR computation & \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} \\\\\nISR variant & Proved-only (ISR$^*$) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation exception.}\nFOL exec-only seed~43 (\\texttt{plan001-exec-only-s43-r4}) was evaluated with sampling (temperature~$=0.7$) rather than greedy decoding.\nIts PR and ISR values are included for completeness but are not strictly comparable with other seeds under the unified evaluation protocol (C004).\n\n\n\\section{STA Algorithm}\n\\label{app:algorithm}\n\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:sta} gives the full procedure for computing ISR over a proved formalization.\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[h]\n\\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{alg:sta}\n\\begin{algorithmic}[1]\n\\REQUIRE Formalization $x = \\{c_1, \\ldots, c_n\\}$, solver $S$\n\\ENSURE ISR score $\\in [0,1]$ or $\\bot$ (undefined)\n\\IF{$S(x) \\neq \\textit{proved}$}\n  \\RETURN $\\bot$\n\\ENDIF\n\\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets 0$\n\\FOR{$i = 1$ \\TO $n$}\n  \\STATE $x' \\gets x \\setminus \\{c_i\\}$\n  \\IF{$S(x') = \\textit{failed}$}\n    \\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets n_{\\text{nec}} + 1$\n  \\ENDIF\n\\ENDFOR\n\\RETURN $n_{\\text{nec}} \\mathbin{/} n$\n\\end{algorithmic}\n\\end{algorithm}\n\n\n\\section{Extended Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{What ISR measures---and what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness---whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning.\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.\nISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.\nISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof---rather than absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$---even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound explains the sole apparent ISR-as-reward success (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}): ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 produces shorter formalizations, mechanically inflating ISR.\nPremise-count-matched comparison eliminates the difference ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nThis bias is not specific to our setting---any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart: implications.}\nThe meta-Goodhart finding extends beyond this specific system.\nISR was designed to \\emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\\text{exec}}$, yet when incorporated into the reward signal, the model games ISR itself---creating a regress where any diagnostic metric risks becoming a target when optimized.\nThe dose-response pattern ($\\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming inflates ISR without genuine improvement; $\\beta{=}0.3$: performance collapse) maps onto the trajectory predicted by~\\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}, but at a meta level: the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of~\\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart---the optimizer enters a regime where the proxy-target relationship breaks down entirely---while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant where the diagnostic is gamed through its own measurement mechanism.\nThe practical implication is that ISR---and structurally similar diagnostics---should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm 47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds show similar divergence: 2/3 dominated by conclusion duplication (H7), 1/3 by premise minimization (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nMulti-seed evaluation with structural diagnostics should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems.\n\n\n\\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}\n\\label{app:hacking-modes}\n\nWe identify seven distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.\nEach mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.\nTable~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rnd} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ in premises & 87 & 17 & 81 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{65} & 0 & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}$ inflated & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nC-w/o-utility & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & -- & -- & -- & -- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed means. Exec: H1${=}87$\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes (H7/Tautological in s44, Repetition in s43, Contradiction at 1000 steps). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.\nThe solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded\nConclusion: flies(tweety).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nVariable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (71--93\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (64.5\\% pooled).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7 --- Conclusion Duplication}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7 is the only mode that simultaneously games both exec reward and ISR reward.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nISR-specific: 64.5\\% pooled (149/231) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%).\n\\textbf{Zero} occurrences under Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ across all three seeds ($0/256$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=14$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous---none individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Observed in Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\\% (3/79).\nNot observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).\n\n\n\\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso quodlibet}.\nIndividual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean\n  % conclusion embedded among premises\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n% NL: \"Fido is happy.\"\n% Model output:\nfido_is_happy.\n:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.\nThe fact is necessarily necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.\nFunctionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).\n95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% non-hacking.\n\n\n\\section{Per-Seed Detailed Results}\n\\label{app:detailed-results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed} and \\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} report full per-seed results.\nAll metrics are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} on the held-out 100-example FOLIO evaluation set using greedy decoding unless otherwise noted.\nISR values are proved-only ISR$^*$.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). Includes the per-seed $\\beta{=}0.1$ breakdown referenced from Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H\\ = percentage among proved samples. ``---'' indicates data not available. $^\\dagger$s43 uses sampling eval ($T{=}0.7$).}\n\\label{tab:fol-detailed}\n\\label{tab:beta01-3seed}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.6$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\\% genuine proofs, but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent solver-trace-ablation\n\n\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{app:setup}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists all hyperparameters shared across experiments.\nAll RL conditions use the same GRPO configuration; only the reward function differs.\nSeeds control model weight initialization and data shuffling; training data is held constant across all seeds and conditions (Constraint~C001).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters for all GRPO experiments.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\footnotesize\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Parameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Model}} \\\\\nBase model & Qwen3-4B \\\\\nPrecision & BF16 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{GRPO Training}} \\\\\nRollouts per prompt ($K$) & 8 \\\\\nTraining steps (default) & 500 \\\\\nTraining steps (extended) & 1000 \\\\\nLearning rate & $1 \\times 10^{-6}$ \\\\\nPer-device batch size & 4 \\\\\nGradient accumulation steps & 2 \\\\\nEffective batch size & 8 \\\\\nMax completion length & 512 tokens \\\\\nRandom seeds & 42, 43, 44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Data}} \\\\\nTraining set & FOLIO, 500 examples \\\\\nEvaluation set & FOLIO, 100 examples (held-out) \\\\\nData file & \\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Solvers}} \\\\\nFOL solver & Prover9 \\\\\nProlog solver & SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Evaluation}} \\\\\nDecoding & Greedy ($T{=}0$) \\\\\nISR computation & \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} \\\\\nISR variant & Proved-only (ISR$^*$) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation exception.}\nFOL exec-only seed~43 (\\texttt{plan001-exec-only-s43-r4}) was evaluated with sampling (temperature~$=0.7$) rather than greedy decoding.\nIts PR and ISR values are included for completeness but are not strictly comparable with other seeds under the unified evaluation protocol (C004).\n\n\n\\section{STA Algorithm}\n\\label{app:algorithm}\n\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:sta} gives the full procedure for computing ISR over a proved formalization.\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[h]\n\\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{alg:sta}\n\\begin{algorithmic}[1]\n\\REQUIRE Formalization $x = \\{c_1, \\ldots, c_n\\}$, solver $S$\n\\ENSURE ISR score $\\in [0,1]$ or $\\bot$ (undefined)\n\\IF{$S(x) \\neq \\textit{proved}$}\n  \\RETURN $\\bot$\n\\ENDIF\n\\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets 0$\n\\FOR{$i = 1$ \\TO $n$}\n  \\STATE $x' \\gets x \\setminus \\{c_i\\}$\n  \\IF{$S(x') = \\textit{failed}$}\n    \\STATE $n_{\\text{nec}} \\gets n_{\\text{nec}} + 1$\n  \\ENDIF\n\\ENDFOR\n\\RETURN $n_{\\text{nec}} \\mathbin{/} n$\n\\end{algorithmic}\n\\end{algorithm}\n\n\n\\section{Extended Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{What ISR measures---and what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness---whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning.\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.\nISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.\nISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof---rather than absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$---even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound explains the sole apparent ISR-as-reward success (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}): ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 produces shorter formalizations, mechanically inflating ISR.\nPremise-count-matched comparison eliminates the difference ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed}).\nThis bias is not specific to our setting---any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart: implications.}\nThe meta-Goodhart finding extends beyond this specific system.\nISR was designed to \\emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\\text{exec}}$, yet when incorporated into the reward signal, the model games ISR itself---creating a regress where any diagnostic metric risks becoming a target when optimized.\nThe dose-response pattern ($\\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming inflates ISR without genuine improvement; $\\beta{=}0.3$: performance collapse) maps onto the trajectory predicted by~\\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}, but at a meta level: the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.\nUnder the taxonomy of~\\cite{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy}, $\\beta{=}0.3$ exemplifies extremal Goodhart---the optimizer enters a regime where the proxy-target relationship breaks down entirely---while $\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant where the diagnostic is gamed through its own measurement mechanism.\nThe practical implication is that ISR---and structurally similar diagnostics---should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm 47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds show similar divergence: 2/3 dominated by conclusion duplication (H7), 1/3 by premise minimization (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nMulti-seed evaluation with structural diagnostics should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems.\n\n\n\\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}\n\\label{app:hacking-modes}\n\nWe identify seven distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.\nEach mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.\nTable~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rnd} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ in premises & 87 & 17 & 81 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{65} & 0 & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}$ inflated & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nC-w/o-utility & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & -- & -- & -- & -- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed means. Exec: H1${=}87$\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes (H7/Tautological in s44, Repetition in s43, Contradiction at 1000 steps). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.\nThe solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded\nConclusion: flies(tweety).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nVariable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (71--93\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (64.5\\% pooled).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7 --- Conclusion Duplication}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7 is the only mode that simultaneously games both exec reward and ISR reward.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nISR-specific: 64.5\\% pooled (149/231) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%).\n\\textbf{Zero} occurrences under Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ across all three seeds ($0/256$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=14$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous---none individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Observed in Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\\% (3/79).\nNot observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).\n\n\n\\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso quodlibet}.\nIndividual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean\n  % conclusion embedded among premises\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n% NL: \"Fido is happy.\"\n% Model output:\nfido_is_happy.\n:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.\nThe fact is necessarily necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.\nFunctionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).\n95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% non-hacking.\n\n\n\\section{Per-Seed Detailed Results}\n\\label{app:detailed-results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:beta01-3seed} and \\ref{tab:prolog-crosslogic} report full per-seed results.\nAll metrics are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} on the held-out 100-example FOLIO evaluation set using greedy decoding unless otherwise noted.\nISR values are proved-only ISR$^*$.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). Includes the per-seed $\\beta{=}0.1$ breakdown referenced from Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H\\ = percentage among proved samples. ``---'' indicates data not available. $^\\dagger$s43 uses sampling eval ($T{=}0.7$).}\n\\label{tab:fol-detailed}\n\\label{tab:beta01-3seed}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{Steps} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{9}{@{}l}{\\textit{Baselines}} \\\\\nBase model (no training)\n  & --- & 0 & 0.12 & 0.064 & --- & --- & --- & Pre-training \\\\\nGround-truth FOL\n  & --- & --- & 0.18 & 0.559 & --- & --- & --- & Human formalizations \\\\\nSFT\n  & 42 & ${\\sim}$8 ep. & 0.14 & 0.571 & 0 & 7.1 & \\textbf{92.9} & Supervised, no RL \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{9}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 100 & 0.32 & \\textbf{0.642} & --- & --- & --- & Early checkpoint \\\\\n  & 42 & 500 & 0.93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87.1 & 12.9 & \\\\\n  & 42 & 1000 & 0.96 & 0.003 & 0 & 100 & 0 & Contradiction explosion \\\\\n  & 43 & 500 & 0.14 & 0$^\\dagger$ & --- & --- & --- & $^\\dagger$Sampling $T{=}0.7$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 500 & 0.98 & 0.582 & 100 & 0 & 0 & Tautological collapse \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-9}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std$^\\dagger$} & & 16.0 & 51.9 & \\textbf{30.9} & Mixed H1+H7 \\\\\n  & 43 & 500 & 0.61 & 0.830 & \\textbf{96.7} & 0 & ${\\sim}$3 & H7 dominant \\\\\n  & 44 & 500 & 0.89 & 0.902 & 86.5 & 0 & ${\\sim}$13.5 & H7 dominant \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-9}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.77{\\pm}0.14$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.716{\\pm}0.26$} & & & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: Prolog (SWI-Prolog 8.4.2). PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/Gen.\\ = percentage among proved samples.  ``---'' indicates data not available.}\n\\label{tab:prolog-detailed}\n\\label{tab:prolog-crosslogic}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{Gen.\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.83 & 0.037 & 0 & 0 & --- & Ground fact enumeration \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.56 & 0.015 & --- & --- & --- & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.21 & 0.387 & --- & --- & --- & Small-sample ($n{=}21$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.53{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.146{\\pm}0.21$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec+ISR (STA reward)}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.50 & 1.000 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & H7 tautological \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.38 & 0.185 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 97.4 & 0 & $^\\ddagger$Functional H7$^a$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.97 & 0.912 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 0 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & Query-as-fact gaming$^b$ \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.62{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.699{\\pm}0.45$} & & 3/3 Goodhart, 0\\% genuine \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\n\\vspace{4pt}\n{\\footnotesize\n$^a$ Strict H7 classifier reports 0\\%, but functional analysis shows all 38/38 proved samples are tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$). Uppercase pseudo-Prolog syntax bypasses the text-match classifier.\\\\\n$^b$ Strict classifier reports Genuine$\\,{=}\\,100$\\%, but 95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$. The model converts NL to snake\\_case atoms serving as both fact and query---a third distinct Goodhart pathway.\\\\\n$^\\ddagger$ Strict classifier value is misleading; see footnotes $^a$/$^b$.\n}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Prolog cross-logic comparison. Exec-only vs Exec+ISR (STA reward) across three seeds, illustrating universal Goodhart under ISR-as-reward (3/3 seeds, 0\\% genuine) and three distinct gaming pathways (H7 tautological, uppercase pseudo-Prolog, query-as-fact).}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.6$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\\% genuine proofs, but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":370,"oldLines":7,"newStart":370,"newLines":7,"lines":[" "," \\begin{table*}[t]"," \\centering","-\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: Prolog (SWI-Prolog 8.4.2).
